Assumption, stated once and everywhere: every card is treated as accepted and ordered in full. Demand is observed (sale_items, unchanged); the inventory trajectory is simulated (engine_on_hand + recommended qty, depleted by real daily units). A window is counted in OPEN days, never calendar days. A pending card is not a failed one and sits in no denominator. Absence is never zero.

Engine Confidence Score

0.0/ 100, insufficient evidence

Definition. Engine Confidence Score = Measured performance (n/a) x Evidence sufficiency (25.6) / 100. It is a product, not an average: a strong-looking performance on almost no evidence collapses toward zero, because we do not know yet, and not knowing is not a middling result. A low score here does not say the engine performs badly. It says the test cannot yet claim that it performs well. Both halves are printed so that cannot be misread. A component with no measurement behind it is left out, never scored 50.

Measured performance: n/a

no scored card yet, so no performance can be measured

The four metrics, per run

Two window modes over two periods. fixed gives every card the same 7-open-day window: comparable, but a reorder sized for three weeks cannot sell through in one, so read velocity and turnover there, not the success rate. intended gives each card the horizon the engine sized it for, which is the honest test of the quantity and is why most of its cards stay pending for weeks. period is cards issued since 2026-10-16; trailing30 is cards issued since 2026-09-22, so earlier cards keep being scored.

Calibration (period, fixed window)

Stated confidence against how often the SKU met its own predicted rate. Scored against the met-forecast rate rather than "successful" because the latter is unreachable whenever the window is shorter than the cover, and a table of zeroes would indict the engine for the measurement's own flaw.

No scored card carries a stated confidence yet.
